## 11. Verification gates and the hostile-reviewer list

### 11.1 Standing tests (all in `cli/tests/guard/` unless noted)

`test_isolation`, `test_network_allowlist`, `test_no_promotion_strings`, `test_regex_safety`, `test_textsafe`, `test_safe_write`, `test_run_git_hardening`, `test_config_trust`, `test_prompt_data_line`, `test_fixture_collection`, `test_registry_invariants`, `test_catalogue_counts`, `test_cmd_modules_import_light`, the fixture runner (3 TP and 3 NEG per check), the SSRF vector suite, the package-guard egress test, the hook JSON-confusion table, the settings-merge idempotency tests, `test_workflows`, `test_launch_files`, `bench/tests`, `bench/precision/tests`, `mcp/tests/test_guard_*.py`.

### 11.2 Gate commands (the lead's own gates, run by G12 in this order)

1. `cd /home/user/Paddock/cli && python -m pytest -x -q -p no:cacheprovider tests` on 3.13 (expect all guard tests green and the existing suite with 0 failures and 0 errors apart from the one known environment failure, `test_zero_third_party_imports`, and no drop in the pass count against the baseline G0 recorded), then the same on 3.11 in `/tmp/guard-py311` (expect no failures).
2. `cd /home/user/Paddock/mcp && python -m pytest -x -q -p no:cacheprovider tests` on mcp 2.3.0, then on 1.30.0 in `/tmp/guard-mcp130`.
3. `cd /home/user/Paddock && python -m pytest -x -q -p no:cacheprovider bench/tests bench/precision/tests`.
4. `ruff check .` (CI rule set; fixtures are excluded by `ruff.toml`).
5. Wheel check: `pip wheel --no-deps -q -w /tmp/guard-wheel ./cli && python3 -m venv /tmp/guard-clean && /tmp/guard-clean/bin/pip install -q /tmp/guard-wheel/paddock_cli-*.whl && /tmp/guard-clean/bin/pip list --format=freeze | grep -v -E '^(pip|setuptools|paddock-cli)=='` prints nothing; `unzip -l /tmp/guard-wheel/paddock_cli-*.whl` lists `paddock_cli/guard/packs/example.toml`, `paddock_cli/guard/pkg/data/top_npm.json`, `top_pypi.json` and `known_legit_near.json`; then, with the apps expanded by `python cli/tests/guard/support/expand_app.py cli/tests/guard/fixtures/apps/clean-app /tmp/guard-apps/clean` (and `vulnerable-app` likewise), `/tmp/guard-clean/bin/paddock-guard --version`, `paddock guard list`, `paddock guard check /tmp/guard-apps/clean --offline --no-cache` (exit 0) and the same on `/tmp/guard-apps/vulnerable` (exit 1).
6. `python -m paddock_cli.guard catalogue --check`; `python scripts/guard_claims_lint.py`; `python bench/precision/gate.py --release` (record result).
7. Isolation proof by hand: `grep -rnE "^(import|from) " cli/paddock_cli/guard | grep -vE "from \.|from __future__|import (os|sys|re|json|...stdlib...)"` reviewed, plus `git diff --stat` against the contract date showing no file outside the ownership map changed.
8. Documents: `mkdocs build --strict` if available; `cd dashboard && npx vitest run tests/brand.test.tsx` if available.
9. `git status --short` must show only files in the ownership map plus the generated data; gate commands run with `--no-cache` or on temporary copies, so they leave nothing inside the tree.
10. `mcp/guard-dist/README.md` contains the `mcp-name:` line and `mcp/guard-dist/pyproject.toml` declares the `paddock-guard-mcp` script (the check G11a does not make).

### 11.3 Hostile-reviewer list

Reviewers must be agents or people who did not write the code under review. Each item: the attack to attempt, and what must be shown. An attack that works is an "important" finding.

1. **SSRF bypasses.** Feed the URL check every spelling of loopback, link-local, metadata and private addresses (decimal, hex, octal, short, mixed, mapped IPv6, NAT64, 6to4, fullwidth and ideographic characters, userinfo and backslash tricks, percent-encoding, trailing dots, zone ids); rebinding resolver; redirects to internal hosts, schemes and ports; cross-origin script URLs; a response that sends 3,000 headers or an endless body; slow-loris. Show: nothing connects to a non-global address, the resolver is called once per hop, limits hold, no proxy is used.
2. **Loopback opt-in escape.** Try to enable loopback from a project config, environment variable, hook payload, MCP argument, redirect, or symlinked user config. Show it cannot.
3. **ReDoS in every regex.** Run `test_regex_safety` plus an independent fuzz (random and structured 4,000 to 200,000 character inputs, including nested-quantifier bait) over every compiled pattern in `checks/`, `pkg/`, `urlcheck/`, `initgen/`, `hooks/` and every pack rule the loader accepts. Show a hard per-pattern bound; show a hostile TOML pack with a catastrophic pattern is rejected by the lint.
4. **Path traversal and symlink escapes in the scanner.** Repositories containing symlinks to `/etc/passwd` and to parent directories, FIFOs, huge files, deep trees, `..` in a faked `git ls-files` listing, hardlinks, and a hostile `git archive` tar. Show nothing outside the root is read, nothing blocks, and every skip is reported.
5. **Hook JSON confusion.** Malformed, oversize, duplicated-key, wrong-typed stdin; unknown events; `tool_input` as a string; file paths with `..`, absolute paths, symlinks; shell command strings built to evade the install parser (`sh -c`, command substitution, here-strings, aliases, line continuations). Show exit codes follow 7.1, no exception escapes, and unparseable installs fall through to CI (stated limit).
6. **settings.json clobbering.** Run `init` on settings files with existing hooks of every event, extra fields, tabs, CRLF, comments, duplicate keys and symlinks. Show nothing the user wrote is lost, re-running changes no byte, `--remove` is exact, and a failed write leaves the original.
7. **Idempotency.** Run every installer (`init --all`, shim, baseline create, pre-commit, rule files) twice and three times; compare sha256 of every touched file and of the directory tree.
8. **Prompt injection through scanned files into MCP, hook and CLI output.** Repositories whose file names, file contents, package names, registry descriptions, rule packs, rules files and MCP configs contain instructions, bidi and zero-width text, ANSI escapes and markdown or HTML. Show: no forbidden character class survives, repo text appears only in `untrusted` or `file` fields, `agent_prompt` contains no repo-derived sentence, project rule packs cannot supply prompt text, tool descriptions are constant. Record the residual filename-as-sentence risk.
9. **Secret exfiltration through findings.** Plant secrets of every family in every file type, in findings' `evidence`, in fixers' diffs, in the ledger, in `last-report.json`, in SARIF, in the PR comment, in MCP results and in error messages. Show that no run reveals any character or hash of a secret beyond the declared public prefix (no `value_hash` exists any more, D10), and that nothing is sent by the package guard or URL check beyond 5.1 and 6.3.
10. **Network egress.** With a recording transport and with `strace -f -e trace=network` where available, run the whole CLI against a repository of secrets. Show the only destinations are the four package hosts, the single user-named URL host, GitHub (PR comment), and nothing else; show `--offline` opens no socket.
11. **Suppression, baseline and config dodges.** An agent adds `paddock:ignore`, edits `.paddock/guard.toml`, grows the baseline, deletes the flagged file, or moves the flagged code. Show: non-suppressible checks ignore inline comments, `verify` returns `suppressed` or `verified (file removed)`, the CI comment lists new suppressions, baseline growth and disabled checks, and `--config-ref` keeps the base config in force.
12. **Loop-breaker abuse.** Drive the hook with repeated blocks to make the agent ignore findings; confirm the third repeat downgrades with a visible `hook_ignored_by_agent` ledger event and that CI still blocks.
13. **Fixer safety.** TOCTOU (file changes between plan and apply), symlinked targets, CRLF, mode bits, concurrent runs, literals that appear twice, values containing `$`, backslashes or quotes. Show gated, atomic, and the diff redacts the literal.
14. **Package guard evasion and poisoning.** Cache entries edited by hand, oversize responses, wrong content types, registry returning 200 for everything, names with unicode or path characters, version strings with newlines, `.npmrc` redirecting the registry, look-alike names inside the allow-list. Show each yields `unknown` or the right verdict and never `ok` on partial data; confirm a project config allow-list cannot mark a package `ok`.
15. **Shim bypass and breakage.** `PATH` ordering, a missing real tool, a wrapper for a tool the user does not have, recursion (shim calling itself), `PADDOCK_SHIM_BYPASS`, non-POSIX shells. Show fail-open is visible and uninstall is exact.
16. **Benchmark circularity.** Audit that the primary outcome uses oracles, that oracles share no code or author context with checks, that prompts contain no Guard vocabulary, that `generic_review` exists or the limitation is stated, that feature-absent generations are reported, and that dev and test splits are not mixed. Re-score a sample by hand.
17. **Precision-study leakage.** Verify the freeze files, that `run.py` refuses an unfrozen holdout, that labellers never saw tier, severity or fix text, that corpus selection predates Guard output (hashes and dates), that duplicates are removed, that disputed items are listed, and that the conservative figure is the one used for tiering.
18. **README and docs claims versus reality.** Every number, every "verified", "proves", "checks" and "default" sentence traced to a report, a test or the catalogue; run the claims lint with planted violations; check the hero scoping sentence and the section 12 limits appear; check no "secure" or "safe app" claim and no call to star.
19. **Promotion and telemetry.** Grep for stars, URLs, analytics, update checks, and outbound calls other than 10; run the CLI with a network trap. Show the URL line appears only in human receipts and PR comments and disappears with `PADDOCK_NO_PROMPT=1`.
20. **Relicensing readiness.** Run the isolation test, confirm SPDX headers, `PROVENANCE.md` completeness, no copied third-party rule text, and that moving `cli/paddock_cli/guard/` to a new directory with only `__init__` parent stub still imports and passes its tests.
21. **Contention.** Confirm no edit to `ci.yml`, `release.yml`, `mkdocs.yml` (unless D26 condition), `docs/READINESS.md`, `docs/GROWTH.md`, orchestrator, assurance, agent, intel, schema, verifier; confirm the `main.py` diff is the single hunk.
22. **Hostile repository writes.** Symlinked `.paddock`, `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `.gitignore`, ledger and backup paths, and `--write-rc` with symlinked dotfiles. Show that no write lands outside the root or the state directory.
23. **Git and parser execution.** A hostile `.git/config` (fsmonitor, sshCommand, hooksPath, diff.external) in the scanned tree, in `git archive` output and in corpus entries; refs beginning with `-`; deeply nested JSON, TOML, Python and HTML; huge integers. Show that no process runs and that every case ends bounded with `skipped` and `complete: false`.
24. **Config and trust dodges.** A project config that loosens, a user-level config reached through `PADDOCK_CONFIG_DIR` or `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` into the repository, a symlinked or world-writable user config, `.paddock/guard.toml` in an ancestor directory, a hook root of `$HOME`. Show that each is ignored or refused with a visible notice.
25. **Egress primitives.** `guard_url` and the package tools used by a prompt-injected agent; `GITHUB_API_URL` pointing at an attacker host; a mis-parsed install command that carries a token. Show that nothing token-shaped or secret-derived leaves the machine and that the tools are absent by default.
26. **Evaluation honesty.** Re-derive each reported metric from raw data; check that no metric equals its detector, that generations with `could_read_repo` other than `false` produce no effect figure, that `generic_review` exists for the primary contrast, that sub-rule and per-repository clustering are applied, and that stale `rules_sha` entries fail `gate.py --release`.
27. **Claim surfaces.** Titles, meta descriptions, JSON-LD, registry fields, `llms.txt` and badge text for the hero sentence and for fixture counts; README capability sentences against effective tiers.

---

## 12. Limits and what is explicitly NOT claimed

- Guard does not prove an application is safe. "Verified" means the specific finding disappeared and no new blocking finding appeared in the touched files, as defined in 4.0.5. It means that the pattern is no longer matched, not that the risk is gone: a leaked credential stays compromised until rotated, and a finding can be made to disappear by obfuscation, renaming or moving code (the limits of 4.0.5).
- It is static analysis plus registry facts. It does not run the app, probe a live backend, test authorisation between users, or verify RLS behaviour (a behavioural RLS oracle with throwaway Postgres is deferred, D24). The URL check observes what a browser would receive; it does not test the server.
- Detection is bounded: a lexer and an AST-based taint approximation, not a full data-flow engine; JavaScript has no parser; YAML is read line by line. Precision is measured, recall is not claimed. Recall against oracle-detected vulnerabilities is reported in the benchmark with its caveats and nothing more.
- Tier labels are measured on a small public-code corpus labelled by AI agents of one model family with no human audit. Several checks are expected to ship as `medium` with `insufficient-n`. Claims about precision apply only to checks whose `tiers.json` entry is `measured`, and only on that corpus.
- The benchmark uses one model family (the session's own, through subagents), small N, prompts and oracles written by AI agents, and a repair loop that gives Guard extra turns; `generic_review` addresses that if it is run. It shows association within this setup, not general effectiveness, and cannot show behaviour with other models or real users.
- Package guard: `unknown` is a real outcome; typosquat detection is heuristic (medium tier, warn only); malicious-package detection is by OSV advisory name only (content-level detection cannot be measured); transitive dependencies get only the OSV malicious check; yarn and pnpm lockfiles are not parsed; no ecosystems beyond npm and PyPI; private registries are not checked.
- The install shim is POSIX only, can be bypassed, and does not see `python -m pip`; the hook install parser is best effort. The backstop is the CI dependency diff, and CI can be disabled by whoever controls the repository.
- Hooks are Claude Code only in this pass. Cursor, Copilot, Windsurf, Codex, Zed and Cline integrations that are marked UNVERIFIED (7.9) are not claimed to work until verified against the real tools.
- The ledger and receipts are local and editable; they are not tamper-evident and do not interoperate with the P3 attestation format.
- Hooks, the shim and rule files are advisory: an agent can bypass the hook after two reminders, the shim can be bypassed, and CI is the only enforcement and can itself be disabled by whoever controls the repository (D33).
- The precision study measures public libraries, templates and apps, not AI-generated apps, with AI labellers of one model family whose errors are correlated and no human audit; its intervals are not coverage guarantees, and tiers are labelled `ai-estimated` until a human audit is recorded. The benchmark prompts were selected to elicit the targeted classes, the oracles share a definition source and a model family with the checks, and no population rate is claimed.
- Package names and versions of public dependencies are disclosed to npm, PyPI and OSV; private and workspace names are never sent. Results depend on registry availability and say `unknown` otherwise.
- Results depend on the Python version only for syntax the running interpreter cannot parse; such files are skipped and reported. Network tools of the MCP server are opt-in; when enabled, an allowed host still receives requests.
- Project configuration can only tighten (D32); the state directory is local and not tamper-evident; badges are self-reported local snapshots; the registry, `uvx` and `guard` extra paths do not work until PyPI publishing is set up (OPEN-4).
- Gate B (real users installing unprompted) is not measured, and there is no telemetry to measure it.
- Release signing, SBOM, provenance, PyPI trusted publishing, the Smithery build, the MCP registry publication and the Marketplace flow are UNVERIFIED-IN-SANDBOX: written, structure-tested, never run here.
- Windows is not supported in this pass (shim, hooks tested on POSIX).
- The hero sentence is the owner's; the first-screen scoping sentence is required (D28), and OPEN-3 remains with the owner.
- Dashboard: copy only; no new UI, no change to the design baseline.
